.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-5-## Scope and row selection
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-6-
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-7-- Require an authenticated tenant, resolved Site UUID/code, domain and selected half-open UTC window `[from,to)`. The W1 producer is mini-grid dispatch. Resolve the Site within the tenant and use the established 401/403/404 identity rules. Do not silently expand to another Site, another domain or the mock's global Findings area. Echo the requested window and display timezone; changing dates makes a new scoped read, not a client-side filter of an old list.
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-8-- Start with persisted S4-01 Finding and immutable FindingRevision records, never a UI seed, S3 interval alone, Lab private state or a financial consequence. Select the latest permitted revision of each canonical Site Finding from one consistent S3/S4 basis. A row qualifies only when its selected episode intersects the window (`episode_start < to` and `episode_end > from`); a boundary touch does not qualify. Apply S4 merge canonicalization: one row for the surviving ID, with superseded IDs available as relationship/history links. Extension changes the revision on the same Finding; separated recurrence uses a new Finding ID and recurrence link. Closed and unconfirmed episodes remain eligible historical rows in an intersecting window; the default list is not an implicit open-only filter.
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-9-- Row identity is `(tenant_id, site_id, finding_id)` with the stable Finding UUID, canonical ID, exact selected `finding_revision_id`, domain, episode bounds, lifecycle/transition, selected assessment/run/model/cohort and cited accepted-evidence/configuration IDs. Include limitations and exact Site detail URL `/sites/{site_code}/findings/{finding_id}?revision_id={revision_id}`; the API may also return an unpinned canonical URL. The selected revision link must survive reload and later revision creation. A row's source and analytic lineage is the selected immutable revision's lineage, not a recomputation from its title or latest unrelated consequence.

.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-15-## Watermark and currentness
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-16-
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-17-- Return a read-level selection token/basis ID, accepted-source watermark, S3 assessment selection and analytic watermark, S4 matcher/selection watermark, model/matcher version, `built_at`, coverage/limitations and a typed status. A transactionally consistent scoped read or atomically published projection is acceptable. `knowledge_at` is the stated selection cutoff; do not promise arbitrary commit-ordered historical replay from the current S3 worker clock. Exact revision IDs are the audit anchors.
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-18-- `CURRENT` requires complete scoped discovery and canonical matching through the selected accepted-evidence/configuration/model basis, with no affected dirty, leased or failed S3/S4 work, no watermark mismatch and no moving selection. Relevant late evidence or changed configuration/model invalidates affected windows and must rebuild or make them stale. `PENDING` means no usable completed selection yet; `STALE_PROJECTION` retains prior rows/IDs with the reason while a newer basis is unresolved; `FAILED` reports failed work and any prior selected rows as historical. `UNASSESSED` means no completed assessment/discovery basis. None of these statuses authorizes a current count, current empty claim or an inferred Healthy state. A source-coverage limitation may coexist with a current, complete assessment of the available evidence; state the limitation instead of calling the physical world complete.
.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md-19-- Keep list freshness separate from each row's assessment claim and from money eligibility. A current list can include an `UNCONFIRMED` Finding or a row with no current amount. A stale list cannot silently replace its selected revision links with newer detail heads.

tasks/README.md-69-The dependency registry carries `I-G-01`–`I-G-05` as unscored technical packet nodes. They have no reviewed path to an approved W1 core panel, so `next-work` cannot select them through current core scoring. They are listed separately in its report and activate by `D-2026-10-13-g-gap-packet-activation`: after the active V-01b closes and merges, G-01 takes the next free single-active slot, then G-02 takes the next free slot after reviewed and merged G-01 and merged/accepted V-01a, ahead of an ordinary ranked selection once any checker refusal or owner stop is resolved. G-03–G-05 wait for their reviewed merged UI and packet prerequisites; run at the first free slot with no ready active-wave core candidate, or take their remaining backlog to W1-exit review. A ≤5% ranking contest still goes to the owner before activating a due G packet; a 5–10% notice is recorded. This ordering needs no new owner choice and gives no G node a demo value. The screen ledger's `gap_backfill_task` is a planning pointer, not a delivered gap ID. G-01 introduces the catalogue schema and one real tagged Readiness gateway-firmware missing state; G-02 enrolls the four V-01a screens for complete non-core/open `gap_ids` coverage, then later batches enroll their merged UI.

They are listed separately in its report and activate by `D-2026-10-13-g-gap-packet-activation`: after the active V-01b closes and merges, G-01 takes the next free single-active slot, then G-02 takes the next free slot after reviewed and merged G-01 and merged/accepted V-01a, ahead of an ordinary ranked selection once any checker refusal or owner stop is resolved. G-03–G-05 wait for their reviewed merged UI and packet prerequisites; run at the first free slot with no ready active-wave core candidate, or take their remaining backlog to W1-exit review. The screen ledger's `gap_backfill_task` is a planning pointer, not a delivered gap ID. G-01 introduces the catalogue schema and one real tagged Readiness gateway-firmware missing state; G-02 enrolls the four V-01a screens for complete non-core/open `gap_ids` coverage, then later batches enroll their merged UI.

tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-15-
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-16-## Write, matching and lineage contract
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-17-
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-18-- Allocate `finding.id` and immutable `finding_key` once for tenant, Site, mini-grid, generator asset and candidate-support episode. Persist the first accepted candidate anchor, selected public cohort, supported bounds/window IDs and matcher version. Never key ongoing identity from a mutable first-ON time, UTC day, assessment hash or private Lab run.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-19-- Match a CURRENT selected candidate span to persisted episodes across UTC-day boundaries. Supported overlap or a gap ≤20 minutes may extend one episode only when every instant of the gap has observed coherent segment coverage: Necessary or Indeterminate for documented modelling uncertainty only, with no missing/contradictory/ambiguous input. Window coverage or an empty segment list is insufficient. A supported separation >20 minutes starts a new Finding; link `recurs_after_finding_id` only when an observed generator OFF establishes the break, otherwise leave the recurrence relation unknown. Late earlier accepted evidence may move the bound/first-ON key without renaming the Finding.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-20-- On a split, retain the original ID for the part containing its immutable anchor, append a revision explaining changed support, and link a new Finding for each other supported part. On a later bridge between two persisted IDs, preserve both old URLs/revisions, choose the earlier stable anchor (then UUID) as canonical, record a durable merge/supersession relation, and show only the canonical active episode. Do not silently delete or reuse either ID.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-21-- Persist Finding and append-only FindingRevision. Idempotency is `(finding, canonical selected assessment basis, transition)`: retry of one basis adds no revision. Each revision cites exact selected interval AssessmentRevision, its AnalyticRun/model/cohort/window and selected window AssessmentRevision; cross-window episodes also cite every supporting interval revision, using a relation when one FK is insufficient. Keep the technical design's primary assessment FK. Preserve the complete analytic input set separately from accepted evidence IDs/digests actually cited by candidate segments, plus as-accepted Foundation/binding and selected control revisions, source/batch/envelope lineage.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-22-- Phase-1 clarification `D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-1-clarifications`: use unique `(finding_id,revision_no)` and `(finding_id,basis_sha256,transition)` rather than the technical design's insufficient primary-assessment uniqueness; include selected window and interval revision IDs, cited evidence, control/binding provenance, bounds/limitations and rule versions in the canonical basis. A provenance-only revision is `BASIS_REFRESHED`, while a changed supported span/claim/limit is `SUPPORT_CHANGED`. Phase 2 must show the selected window basis for every revision and preserve old pinned lineage.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-23-- A changed accepted basis, candidate duration/limit, backdated control or reviewed model version appends a revision. Historical `dispatch-3` may be pinned for audit but cannot create/refresh a CURRENT `dispatch-3.1` Finding; later reviewed `dispatch-3.x` is a new basis, never a relabel. An Indeterminate CURRENT reassessment may append `UNCONFIRMED` to an existing episode but creates no new avoidability Finding. A complete CURRENT no-Candidate/Necessary-only reassessment can append `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED`; it does not say repaired or savings realised. STALE/FAILED/UNASSESSED changes read status without manufacturing a revision.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-24-- Phase-1 fix clarification `D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-1-fix-clarifications`: after `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED`, a current Indeterminate basis may append `UNCONFIRMED` while preserving the prior closure in history; a Candidate on the same unsupported span without proven separation appends `REOPENED` to the same Finding. A ≤20-minute bounded stop may bridge episode identity only with ordered accepted OFF reports at both ends and no contrary report; it adds no Candidate minutes. Recurrence needs assessed separation plus an OFF after the prior run and before the new run. Merge canonical choice remains earliest stable anchor/UUID even when the anchor is only historical.
tasks\S4-01-finding-identity.md-25-- Phase-2 clarification `D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-2-read-boundaries`: the stop-bridge proof must inspect typed accepted generator-state records from the exact selected AnalyticRun over the stop, under the same fence, because the interval result alone hides an intervening contradiction. A contradictory/non-GOOD/wrong-cohort record vetoes the bridge; if full proof is unavailable, disable stop bridging and name the limit. Include endpoint/intervening evidence and `claim.continuity`/`claim.recurrence` facts in the canonical Finding basis; preserve stored revisions under a new matcher version if the basis rule changes after persistence.

# A-finding-index - independent review (fresh Claude reviewer)

Date: 2026-10-15
Reviewed: branch `plan/A-finding-index`, HEAD `002f8cfe` ("A-finding-index resolved"), base `789d76d1`.
Inputs: `.agent/architect-A-finding-index-request.md`, `.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md`, `D-2026-10-15-a-finding-index` (`.ai/DECISIONS.md`), registry diff, `tasks/S4-04-site-finding-index.md`, `tasks/S5-02-overview-finding-list.md`, related decisions, v69 `sitefindings` mock, S4-01 code on this base, S5-01 read on `task/S5-01-site-overview` (via `git show`).

## Verdict: RETURN (one Medium; fix is a short contract amendment)

The contract is otherwise sound and implementable. One Medium fail-closed gap: the decision never applies the existing S3 performance-cutover source gate to the index rows/zero claim.

## Findings

### M1 (Medium, acceptance/contract gap) - S3 source gate missing from index currentness

- Where: `.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md:18` (currentness) and `:28` (money); `.ai/DECISIONS.md` `D-2026-10-15-a-finding-index`.
- What: "source-open" appears only as a money gate ("unresolved source-open state means no current amount"). The currentness rules for `CURRENT` (complete discovery, no dirty/leased/failed work, no watermark mismatch) do not mention the Site's S3 performance-cutover gate.
- Why it matters: on this base `backend/assetops_backend/finding_api.py:86-90,104-107,116-117` refuses with 409 `PERFORMANCE_CUTOVER_CLOSED` when the gate is closed: "no current Finding or no-Finding verdict is shown. An exact revision_id reads audit material." The S5-01 read on `task/S5-01-site-overview` (`site_overview/read.py`) does the same: it drops to `STALE_PROJECTION`, chooses no unpinned Finding (`SOURCE_CLOSED_NO_UNPINNED_FINDING`) and allows `ABSENT` only on an open source. As written, an S4-04 implementer can return `CURRENT` positive rows, or a `CURRENT` zero-row "no Findings", for a closed-source Site. That contradicts Finding detail, which would then 409 or show audit-only, and contradicts S5-01 on the same Site/day. S5-02 would then show list rows next to an S5-01 card that says no current Finding.
- Fix: add one rule to the answers file and the decision. A closed S3 source gate (whatever reviewed form it takes, including S4-05 stored provenance once merged) forbids `CURRENT` and any zero-row claim. Either refuse the way the existing Finding routes do (409 `PERFORMANCE_CUTOVER_CLOSED`), or return the rows as audit-only/historical with exact revision links and the gate's reason, and say which. Add a closed-gate variant to the `D-site-finding-index-empty` fixture list (alongside unassessed/dirty/failed) and the matching registry acceptance target.

### L1 (Low) - discovery fence for multi-day and boundary-crossing windows not pinned

- `answers:17-18`: "complete scoped discovery" is not tied to a concrete set of analytic windows. On this base, completeness is per UTC-day analytic window (`postgres_finding_store.match_state`: `analytic_run_selection.seq` vs `finding_match_pass.selection_seq`). An episode that intersects `[from,to)` can be anchored in an earlier day through the 20-minute continuity bridge. The Planner should require every intersecting UTC-day window and its continuity neighbours to be matched through their current selection seq (as LR-01 requeue scope already does) before `CURRENT`. The Planner should also bound the selectable window length, since page size is bounded but window span is not.

### L2 (Low) - "reconcile `findings_over`" could break existing consumers

- `answers:32`: the helper's inclusive end and ascending order are correctly identified as non-conforming (`postgres_finding_store.py:418-430`). It is also consumed by `scheduling/health.py:252`, `scheduling/upstream.py:133` and `analytics/finding_read.py:617`, which S5-01 extends. The S4-04 packet should add a new index read, or prove those consumers unaffected, rather than change the helper's semantics in place.

### L3 (Low) - S5-01 card and index head should provably agree

- Ordering (episode_start desc, Finding UUID asc) matches `D-2026-10-15-s501-finding-choice` and S5-01's `choose()` (greatest start, smallest `str(uuid)`). PostgreSQL uuid byte order equals lowercase-hex string order, so they agree. Recommend that S4-04/S5-02 include a test showing that, for the same basis, the first index row equals the S5-01 card Finding.

### L4 (Low, housekeeping) - stale planning text and anchor style

- `tasks/README.md:9` still says `A-finding-index` "remain[s] open", and `tasks/S4-04-site-finding-index.md:14` still says "Decide `A-finding-index`". The Planner should refresh both at S4-04 refinement.
- The registry evidence uses the lowercase anchor `#d-2026-10-15-a-finding-index`, while sibling nodes use `#D-...`. Cosmetic only, since decisions are not headings.

## What was verified

Canonicalization, latest revision selection, the per-window matcher watermark and the strict half-open overlap can all be built without new architecture. The exact detail URL with `revision_id` already exists (`finding_api.py:110-135`).
- Fail-closed honesty: apart from M1, a zero-row claim requires complete current discovery. PENDING/STALE_PROJECTION/FAILED/UNASSESSED never authorize a count or empty claim. Positive rows come only from persisted immutable revisions, with mock F-108/F-109 excluded. A stale list keeps its selected revision links.
- Stable IDs and ordering: the row key is `(tenant, site, finding_id)`; there is no ordering by money or arrival time; the cursor is bound to the basis and invalidated on change; the provisional priority tuple is withdrawn until a producer exists.
- Consistency: matches `D-2026-10-15-s501-finding-choice` (half-open, canonical-only, no lifecycle/money filter, same tie-break) and `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview` (mini:3 belongs to S4-04/S5-02). LR-01 `money_eligibility` and S4-05 exact-revision provenance are consumed only for the optional amount cell. If S4-04 starts before those merge, the cell stays unavailable. Mixed/unknown source and withheld/pending eligibility show no current amount, consistent with `D-2026-10-15-lr01-source-time-cohort` and `D-2026-10-15-s405-stored-demo-provenance`.
- Mock: the decision correctly excludes the v69 `sitefindings` lens chips (All/Cost/Risks/Opportunities), the "N open" count and the "Manage in Findings" global link, none of which have producers.
- Registry: `node frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs` -> "Registry valid: 228 nodes, 11 linked pilot panels", exit 0. `--next-work --json` -> `status: ranked`, not refused, no global stops. - No owner acceptance is claimed: the decision, answers and registry targets all state that the owner walkthrough and copy acceptance remain pending.

Not run: no test suites (out of scope for a no-code architecture review).
# Architect answer - Site FindingIndex (2026-10-15)

Decision: `D-2026-10-15-a-finding-index`. Resolve `A-finding-index` for the W1 Site read only. Authority is the owner-absence work order item 9, the reviewed S4-01 episode contract, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`, and `D-2026-10-15-s501-finding-choice`. This decision defines a reusable row contract; it does not implement an estate/global index, cold-chain or EV analytics, or approve UI copy.

## Scope and row selection

- Require an authenticated tenant, resolved Site UUID/code, domain and selected half-open UTC window `[from,to)`. The W1 producer is mini-grid dispatch. Resolve the Site within the tenant and use the established 401/403/404 identity rules. Do not silently expand to another Site, another domain or the mock's global Findings area. Echo the requested window and display timezone; changing dates makes a new scoped read, not a client-side filter of an old list.
- Start with persisted S4-01 Finding and immutable FindingRevision records, never a UI seed, S3 interval alone, Lab private state or a financial consequence. Select the latest permitted revision of each canonical Site Finding from one consistent S3/S4 basis. A row qualifies only when its selected episode intersects the window (`episode_start < to` and `episode_end > from`); a boundary touch does not qualify. Apply S4 merge canonicalization: one row for the surviving ID, with superseded IDs available as relationship/history links. Extension changes the revision on the same Finding; separated recurrence uses a new Finding ID and recurrence link. Closed and unconfirmed episodes remain eligible historical rows in an intersecting window; the default list is not an implicit open-only filter.
- Row identity is `(tenant_id, site_id, finding_id)` with the stable Finding UUID, canonical ID, exact selected `finding_revision_id`, domain, episode bounds, lifecycle/transition, selected assessment/run/model/cohort and cited accepted-evidence/configuration IDs. Include limitations and exact Site detail URL `/sites/{site_code}/findings/{finding_id}?revision_id={revision_id}`; the API may also return an unpinned canonical URL. The selected revision link must survive reload and later revision creation. A row's source and analytic lineage is the selected immutable revision's lineage, not a recomputation from its title or latest unrelated consequence.

## Ordering and pagination

- Do not infer a priority or confidence rank from the v6.9 illustrative rows. W1 default order is selected `episode_start` descending, then stable Finding UUID ascending. This replaces the provisional `(priority_tuple, updated_at, finding_id)` ordering in the pre-S3 guidance until a reviewed priority producer exists. A future reviewed priority rule may add a versioned `priority_tuple`; until then any priority/lens control without a supported producer is hidden or explicitly unavailable. Never order by mutable amount or arrival time. Return a deterministic total count and opaque cursor bound to tenant, Site, domain, window, selected basis/watermark and last sort key. A changed basis invalidates the cursor and requires a fresh first page; it must not splice different revision sets together. Page size is bounded. S5-02 consumes the same ordered Site rows and does not independently rank or select an S5-01 card Finding.

## Watermark and currentness

- Return a read-level selection token/basis ID, accepted-source watermark, S3 assessment selection and analytic watermark, S4 matcher/selection watermark, model/matcher version, `built_at`, coverage/limitations and a typed status. A transactionally consistent scoped read or atomically published projection is acceptable. `knowledge_at` is the stated selection cutoff; do not promise arbitrary commit-ordered historical replay from the current S3 worker clock. Exact revision IDs are the audit anchors.
- `CURRENT` requires complete scoped discovery and canonical matching through the selected accepted-evidence/configuration/model basis, with no affected dirty, leased or failed S3/S4 work, no watermark mismatch and no moving selection. Relevant late evidence or changed configuration/model invalidates affected windows and must rebuild or make them stale. `PENDING` means no usable completed selection yet; `STALE_PROJECTION` retains prior rows/IDs with the reason while a newer basis is unresolved; `FAILED` reports failed work and any prior selected rows as historical. `UNASSESSED` means no completed assessment/discovery basis. None of these statuses authorizes a current count, current empty claim or an inferred Healthy state. A source-coverage limitation may coexist with a current, complete assessment of the available evidence; state the limitation instead of calling the physical world complete.
- Keep list freshness separate from each row's assessment claim and from money eligibility. A current list can include an `UNCONFIRMED` Finding or a row with no current amount. A stale list cannot silently replace its selected revision links with newer detail heads.

## Independent fixture proof

- `D-site-finding-index-empty`: persist a tenant/Site/domain/window with an accepted-source and S3/S4 selection watermark, complete assessed discovery, zero canonical intersecting rows and a `CURRENT` zero-row response. Prove a wrong Site/window is excluded and a boundary-touch episode is excluded. Separately exercise unassessed, dirty/stale and failed reads, each visibly Unknown/pending rather than a no-Findings assertion. A filtered empty page after a positive unfiltered read is not the zero-row fixture.
- `D-site-finding-index-rows`: use committed accepted evidence and S4-01 revisions to persist at least one intersecting canonical Finding, ideally multiple for order and cursor proof. Verify stable ID, latest selected revision, exact detail/history link after reload, recurrence versus extension and canonical merge behavior, cross-Site isolation, date change, boundary intersection and invalidation after a late revision. Empty and positive fixtures are separate W1 closeouts; both must be reviewed. Do not count the mock's F-108 or F-109 as produced Findings without their own reviewed analytics.

## Money and provenance

- A row's existence, order, lifecycle and links are independent of money. The optional consequence cell may use only the exact selected FindingRevision and compatible selected-window consequence. Consume the shared LR-01 `money_eligibility` state and S4-05 exact accepted-ID publication provenance when those reviewed but currently unmerged branches become the build baseline. `PENDING_REASSESSMENT` or `WITHHELD_GENERATOR_SERIES`, mixed/unknown source, stale list or consequence, wrong revision/window, unpriced quantity/price or unresolved source-open state means no current amount. Preserve an old value only behind an explicit historical/audit link. Coherent authored or registered simulation must carry its truthful label and still pass exact-head, currentness and quantity/price gates. Do not aggregate, sum overlapping Findings, infer savings, or turn missing price into zero. If S4-04 starts before those branches merge, leave the consequence cell unavailable and add the reviewed contracts before any current-money presentation or S5-02 integration.

## S4-04 and S5-02 handoff

S4-04 builds the authenticated Site/window index read or equivalent projection, affected-work invalidation, stable cursor and exact revision links, then the Site Findings route/list with selected scope, loading/stale/failed/empty/positive states and keyboard navigation. Reconcile the existing `findings_over` helper: its inclusive end comparison and ascending order do not implement this decision. Its selected revisions must share a consistent matcher/assessment fence; a helper alone is not proof of complete discovery. Compare the supported Site screen at desktop and narrow widths, but do not copy unsupported mock rows, global-list links, Cost/Risks/Opportunities filters, priority/confidence badges or open counts without real producers. The reviewer verifies both fixtures independently, same-basis pagination, tenant isolation, reload and exact drill-down. Owner walkthrough and copy acceptance remain pending until performed.

S5-02 consumes this S4-04 contract on the S5-01 selected Site/window and preserves its own scope, watermark and exact row links. It proves `mini:3#a` honest empty/Unknown and `mini:3#b` positive rows independently. S5-01 may retain a known selected Finding card link but does not close either list variant. S4-03 Evidence/History, `mini:1/2/4` and S3 dispatch do not acquire this index as a prerequisite.

backend\tests\dispatch_3_decision_table.md-89-
backend\tests\dispatch_3_decision_table.md:90:`analytics/performance_comparison.py`. A legacy claim is compared with the one selected `dispatch-3.1` interval overlapping its running span. A selected interval of another model version (`STALE_MODEL`) or one whose window has a PENDING, LEASED or FAILED recompute job (`STALE_PROJECTION`) is `UNPROVEN`, even when the claim levels match. `MATCH` also requires every cited record to be accepted and in the selected interval's evidence (a comparable basis; default built, `.agent/S3-01-questions-4.md`). A cited record not accepted centrally is `MISSING_SOURCE`, which keeps the gate closed; it is not parity. A claim change is `INTENTIONAL_DELTA` only through a documented, claim-specific rule difference below. The selected interval's own segments must name the limitation, and every record the legacy claim cites must be accepted and in that interval's evidence. A limitation elsewhere in the window does not qualify. Anything else is `UNPROVEN`. There is no catch-all.

4127-Action state is derived from an append-only, server-ordered `ActionEvent` ledger. Record stable event ID, Action and Site, event type, `occurred_at`, server `recorded_at`, idempotency key, actor kind/ID, source channel, authorization basis and triggering command/job or content revision. Idempotent identical retries return the event; conflicting reuse and cross-Site references fail. Historical reads select a recorded cutoff and then `(recorded_at,id)` order; a backdated `occurred_at` is displayed as such but never rewrites the audit order. `PROPOSED`/`ACCEPTED`, application `PENDING`/`APPLIED`/`FAILED`, and later verification status are separate dimensions. Acceptance does not assert application; an applied change or completed work does not assert benefit. A failed application preserves its attempt, error and retry relation without an effective-policy claim. No human author, approval or attestation is inferred from a gateway credential, client field, local OS user or SYSTEM fixture. Under the existing `A-actor-identity` rule, human writes need a verified operator principal; otherwise only a clearly labelled, server-authorized SYSTEM demonstration command with stable service/job provenance is allowed, or the write stays unavailable.

Validate the expected old revision and Site scope under the publish lock; reject stale, unauthorized or invalid edits and record a failed attempt without claiming the new setting took effect. `APPLIED` requires confirmation of the persisted new effective revision; an ActionEvent alone is insufficient. The old basis and every event/change revision remain addressable after reload. Do not mutate a Lab-only override or rewrite accepted evidence. S6-01's Execution/History and applied `changes:1#a` show policy activity with event and configuration provenance, including pending/failed cases; they do not show an effect verdict.

S2-01c owns `I-edge-fault-scenarios`; S3-01 owns `I-sim-dispatch-cases`; S6-02 owns `I-sim-verification-cases`. `A-real-peer-source` records simulated-only demo input; the first real pilot records its physical peer before refining an adapter task.

    def source_gate(code: str, tenant_id: UUID, site_id: UUID) -> dict[str, Any]:
        try:
            permitted, reason = gate(code, tenant_id, site_id)
        except down as error:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail={"code": "FINDING_STORE_UNAVAILABLE",
                                                         "message": str(error)}) from error
        return {"permitted": permitted, "reason": reason}

    def closed(source: dict[str, Any]) -> HTTPException:
        return HTTPException(status_code=409, detail={
            "code": "PERFORMANCE_CUTOVER_CLOSED", "read": "CLOSED", "message": source["reason"],
            "note": "The Finding's authority is the selected S3 assessment, which is closed for this Site; no "
                    "current Finding or no-Finding verdict is shown. An exact revision_id reads audit material."})

    def served(work: Callable[[], Any], missing: str) -> Any:
        try:
            body = work()
        except down as error:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail={"code": "FINDING_STORE_UNAVAILABLE",
                                                         "message": str(error)}) from error
        if body is None:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=missing)
        return jsonable_encoder(body)

    @router.get("/api/sites/{site_code}/finding-windows/{day}")
    def window_state(request: Request, site_code: str, day: date) -> dict[str, Any]:
        code, tenant_id, site_id = open_scope(request, site_code)
        source = source_gate(code, tenant_id, site_id)
        if not source["permitted"]:
            raise closed(source)
        return {"site_code": code, "access_basis": access_basis, "source_gate": source,
                **served(lambda: reads.window_state(tenant_id, site_id, day), "No such window.")}

    @router.get("/api/sites/{site_code}/findings/{finding_id}")
    def finding(request: Request, site_code: str, finding_id: UUID, revision_id: UUID | None = None,
                after_revision_no: int = Query(0, ge=0), limit: int = Query(200, ge=1, le=500)) -> dict[str, Any]:
        code, tenant_id, site_id = open_scope(request, site_code)
        source = source_gate(code, tenant_id, site_id)
        if not source["permitted"] and revision_id is None:
            raise closed(source)
        body = served(lambda: reads.finding(tenant_id, site_id, finding_id, revision_id,
                                            after_revision_no=after_revision_no, limit=limit,
                                            source_open=source["permitted"]),
                      "No such Finding or revision for this Site.")
        performance = {"status": "OPEN" if source["permitted"] else "CLOSED", "reason": source["reason"]}
        if not source["permitted"]:
            performance["note"] = CLOSED_LINKS

                         "a current claim and its currentness is not read."}

S5-02 reuses the Site/window rows for both `mini:3` variants after S4-04 and S5-01 review; S4-03, S5-01's other panels and S3 dispatch do not wait on the index. Full contract and implementation handoff: `.agent/architect-A-finding-index-answers.md`. This decision grants no owner UI copy or walkthrough acceptance and does not implement a global or cross-domain index.
